#2796. Week 4 Look Ahead: Luka Dončić continues to dominate, while Kon Knueppel is back on the injury report

After Sunday's games, the 2026-27 NBA season will have completed its first three weeks. While devastating injuries dominated early on, historic performances have taken center stage more recently. San Antonio's Victor Wembanyama had a night rarely seen in NBA history, while Lakers star Luka Dončić is on an extreme heater. Let's look back at some of Week 3's key storylines and look ahead to what's to come in Week 4.

- Luka Dončić has been on a historic heater: With LeBron James no longer in the picture, the Lakers became Luka's "team" for those interested in that kind of conversation. And it also opened things up for him to do even more offensively, if that were even possible. Well, it was, and Dončić has been on a run rarely seen in NBA history.

Through nine games, the Lakers guard is averaging 40.1 points, 7.8 rebounds, 7.7 assists, 1.7 steals and 5.8 three-pointers per game while shooting 49.8 percent from the field and 87.3 percent from the foul line. Sure, Dončić is also averaging 4.9 turnovers per game, but his overall production has minimized the impact on his fantasy value.

Not only has he scored at least 40 points in four straight games, but Luka has also tallied at least 35 points, five rebounds and five assists in each of his last seven appearances. At this rate, Dončić is well on his way to being the most valuable player in fantasy basketball...and possibly league MVP as well.

- Victor Wembanyama's historic night: Wembanyama's averages aren't on par with Luka's, but he's gotten off to the start that fantasy managers expected, based on his preseason ADP. The Spurs center had a huge night in the team's November 4 win over the Mavericks, tallying 52 points, 24 rebounds, two assists, two steals and eight blocks. Wembanyama joined Bob McAdoo as the only players in league history to finish a game with at least 50 points, 20 rebounds and five blocks since blocked shots became an official stat.

Also, he joined Wilt Chamberlain as the only players to record at least 50 points, 20 rebounds and eight blocks. Wilt achieved that feat on five separate occasions in his storied career. With Wembanyama not turning 23 until January, this may not be the last time he records a stat line like the one he dropped on Wednesday.

- Kristaps Porziņģis stepping away from basketball: Unfortunately, the Porziņģis situation took another turn last week, as the 7-foot-3 center announced that he was stepping away from basketball. He's been dealing with postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome (POTS) since his time with the Celtics, and managing the illness has been a major challenge. Because of Porziņģis' absence, the Warriors have been withholding payment on his contract, and reports suggest the team hopes to trade him for a player who can help immediately.

For his part, Porziņģis said in a statement last week that he's willing to work with the Warriors to reach a solution that satisfies all parties. In his absence, the Warriors have relied more on Charles Bassey, whose contract won't be fully guaranteed until January 10. Undrafted rookie Graham Ike even made two starts early in the season, and Al Horford is providing bench depth.

- Yaxel Lendeborg is establishing himself as the early Rookie of the Year favorite: Porziņģis' absence was a factor in Golden State's three-game losing streak to start the season, but they've been on a roll ever since. Saturday's overtime win over the Magic extended the Warriors' win streak to seven, with Stephen Curry going off for 41 points on 13-of-19 shooting. The future Hall of Fame guard's big night isn't a surprise. However, Lendeborg has established himself, in the eyes of many, as the early favorite for Rookie of the Year.

During the Warriors' ongoing win streak, he has averaged 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds, 3.9 assists, 0.9 steals, 1.0 blocks and 2.1 three-pointers per game, shooting 53.0 percent from the field and 79.5 percent from the foul line. While many teams go young and bet on perceived upside in the draft, the Warriors prioritized adding a player who can help win now, with Curry and Draymond Green in the latter stages of their respective careers. Lendeborg does that, and he's also looked like a potential cornerstone in the post-Curry/Green era, whenever that may be.

- Kon Knueppel makes his season debut...and aggravates his hamstring strain: Injured just before the start of training camp, Knueppel made his first appearance of the season in the Hornets' November 4 loss to the Nets. Unfortunately, he played just under six minutes before aggravating his strained left hamstring and leaving. Knueppel didn't play in Charlotte's November 6 loss to the Celtics, and the Hornets have not provided a return timeline.

Making things worse for the Hornets, Brandon Miller left that game with groin tightness, and neither Moussa Diabaté (quad) nor Dennis Schröder (thigh) played against the Celtics. Naz Reid struggled the last time out, shooting 6-of-17 from the field, and his start in Charlotte has not gone as smoothly as many hoped. But with injuries, the Hornets will need more from Reid.

- Jalen Duren to miss at least two more weeks: The Pistons have been without their starting center since the second game of the season when he suffered a Grade 2 hamstring strain. Duren's status was updated on Sunday, when the Pistons announced he's cleared to start on-court activities and will be re-evaluated in two weeks.

Paul Reed has filled in at center, averaging 12.8 points, 6.2 rebounds, 2.3 assists, 1.5 steals and 1.2 blocks in 29.3 minutes per game. The Pistons don't need Reed to be a prolific scorer, but he has provided solid fantasy value as Duren's replacement. The disappointing part is that John Collins' production has dropped compared to his 2025-26 numbers with the Clippers. He's averaging 9.4 points and 5.9 rebounds per game and seems to be struggling to fully grasp his role in Detroit. The Pistons play three games next week, two against the Wizards, which could be a "get right" opportunity for Collins.

- Thunder reportedly acquire Nique Clifford: Needing depth due to their many injuries, Oklahoma City reportedly acquired Nique Clifford from the Kings for two future second-round picks. After a decent rookie season, Clifford fell out of the Kings' rotation, averaging 6.3 minutes per game this season. The move to Oklahoma City gives him a fresh start, and he's joining a franchise that has developed young players well in the past.

As for the Kings, will they look to add another guard? Kevin Porter Jr.'s name has come up before, and he's still a free agent. Whatever the Kings decide, one thing will remain the same: Darius Acuff Jr. will stay the focal point of their ongoing rebuild.

- The Wizards ended Week 3 without Anthony Davis and Deandre Ayton: Davis missed the Wizards' final two games of the week with a sprained ankle, while Ayton exited Saturday's loss to the Thunder with a lower left leg injury. Alex Sarr played 28 minutes on Saturday, finishing with 15 points, 14 rebounds and one assist. He didn't record any defensive stats, but the third-year center has a higher ceiling when Davis is out.

Bilal Coulibaly started on Saturday and could retain that role, but he went scoreless on 0-of-8 shooting. The Wizards have a surplus of wings, which lends itself to smaller lineups for longer periods if Davis and Ayton can't play on Monday against the Pistons.

- The Nets and Timberwolves have light schedules: Brooklyn and Minnesota are the only teams scheduled to play two games in Week 4. However, the Timberwolves have a silver lining: both games are on light days (Thursday and Saturday). With those games against Dallas and Chicago, low-rostered options like Terrence Shannon Jr. and Bones Hyland may be worth a roll of the dice for streaming purposes.

The Nets are also active on Saturday, but their first game is on Wednesday, the busiest day of the week. Nolan Traoré, who has been more productive than many expected before the season began, loses some luster in fantasy leagues because of the schedule. The lighter schedule helps Mikel Brown Jr., who isn't playing both games of back-to-backs because of the back injury that ended his lone college season early. Also, guard Egor Dëmin (leg) will be re-evaluated at the beginning of the week, and the schedule may open the door for him to return to action.

- The Mavericks will play on Saturday and Sunday: Dallas is the only team with games on Saturday and Sunday to close out Week 4. That means fantasy managers rostering Kyrie Irving have to anticipate him sitting out one of those games, at Cleveland (Saturday) and at Boston (Sunday). Caleb Martin started the most recent game Irving missed, a November 2 loss to the Kings, and accounted for five points and six rebounds in 21 minutes. Whether he starts or comes off the bench, Naji Marshall is a better streaming option than Martin on nights when Irving doesn't play.

1. How many games will Nic Claxton play before he moves into the Bulls' starting lineup?

2. How much longer will the Raptors be without Kawhi Leonard?

3. Will the Celtics stick with a three-man center rotation all season long?

#2795. FSU soccer loses to Virginia in ACC Tournament

No. 10 Florida State women's soccer fell 3-0 to No. 14 Virginia on Sunday night in Tallahassee, ending its run in the ACC Tournament in the first round.

With the loss, Florida State fell to 9-4-3 on the season, dropping its second straight game.

Florida State's starting 11 included Ryder Ajeto, Wrianna Hudson, Shaneil Buckley, Joelle Jung, Peyton McGovern, Nawreen Ahmad, Tanner Strickland, Bridget Mutipula, Janet Okeke, Grace Hoytink and Kate Ockene.

At 13:25, Hudson's shot was saved by Virginia goalkeeper Victoria Safradin, and Hudson followed with another attempt 13 seconds later that went wide right. Buckley also sent a shot wide right at 14:36.

At 19:57, Ockene came up with another save, this time on Giovana Canali.

Florida State earned a corner kick at 30:10, and moments later at 30:49, Ashley Henderson put a shot on frame, but Safradin made the save.

Virginia broke through at 38:09 when Allie Ross scored off an assist from Ella Carter, giving the Cavaliers a 1-0 lead.

The Seminoles entered halftime trailing by one despite holding a 3-0 advantage in corner kicks. Virginia outshot Florida State 5-4 in the opening half, with each goalkeeper recording two saves.

Virginia extended its lead early in the second half. At 51:24, Ockene saved a shot from Carter, but Canali was there to finish the rebound and make it 2-0.

Florida State looked to respond. At 56:41, Okeke's shot was saved by Safradin, and Hudson missed left at 58:23.

Ockene kept the Seminoles within two with saves on Carter at 67:59 and Sarah Flammia at 71:51.

At 76:34, Florida State earned a corner kick, but Virginia countered moments later at 76:47 as Canali scored her second goal of the night off an assist from Sophia Bradley, putting the Cavaliers ahead 3-0.

The Seminoles continued pushing in the closing minutes. At 84:06, Hudson's shot was saved by Safradin, and Florida State followed with back-to-back corner kicks that produced attempts from Hudson and Buckley, but neither found the net.

Virginia finished with 12 shots, including nine on goal, while Florida State recorded 10 shots and four on goal. The Seminoles held a 7-2 advantage in corner kicks but were called offside five times. Ockene finished with six saves, while Hudson led Florida State with six shots, including two on goal.

The Seminoles will now await their postseason fate as they look ahead to the NCAA Tournament.

#2792. 4 Huge Takeaways After Reaves Leads Shorthanded Lakers Past Pistons

The Los Angeles Lakers entered Sunday night's matchup as one of the top teams in the West with arguably the most in-form player in the league leading them. With Luka Doncic sidelined, concerns about L.A.'s chances of beating the Detroit Pistons seemed justified. Surprisingly, the Lakers dispelled all of these doubts.

With a 121-116 win, the Los Angeles Lakers improved to 9-2 on the season, placing first in the West while notching their fifth consecutive win. While Doncic's absence was undoubtedly a factor, Lakers' star Austin Reaves showed exactly what he is capable of.

From establishing a 26-point lead early in the game to defending against the Pistons' furious comeback charge, the Purple and Gold displayed remarkable tenacity and fortitude. Additionally, with a dominant 43-point outing to lead L.A. past Detroit, Reaves showed how deep the Lakers truly are this season.

Reaves' performance was undoubtedly the most noteworthy aspect of Sunday night's outing. However, with several key developments accompanying this, examine four key takeaways from L.A.'s win over Detroit.

The last few seasons have really seen Austin Reaves transform into a star-caliber player. With some spectacular offensive displays early last season as well, Reaves has demonstrated his ability to be a reliable scoring option. Despite this, there appear to be many who doubt it.

After facing some early-season struggles, especially in light of his sizable contract extension, these comments would seem justified. Still, the 28-year-old proved naysayers wrong with his season-high scoring night against the Pistons.

Needless to say, this is an extremely positive sign for the Lakers, who can be over-reliant on Doncic for creation. While Reaves lacks his running mate's passing and playmaking ability, Sunday night's display showed that he can certainly compensate for it with his scoring.

With 43 points, five rebounds, and four assists on 14-27 FG and 4-6 3PT FG, Reaves had it going from everywhere. With some timely three-pointers in the fourth quarter, even while facing some tough defense, the guard showed exactly why he earned the Purple and Gold's trust.

Walker Kessler has arguably been one of the most consistent performers for the Lakers this season outside of Luka Doncic. Having established himself as an elite big man, Kessler continues to dominate as a defensive anchor and a rebounder. Unfortunately, there are some issues that may demand some attention.

On Sunday night, Kessler notched another double-double of 12 points, 16 rebounds, one steal, and three blocks, showcasing his overall involvement in the win. While his 16 rebounds and three blocks are eye-catching, his scoring on 5-10 shooting isn't necessarily a positive sign.

In his debut performance for the Lakers, Walker Kessler notched a monster 26 points on 12-12 shooting from the field. Barring a few outliers, he has maintained this degree of efficiency, but it is worth noting that his shot attempts are wildly inconsistent.

In games played with Luka Doncic, Kessler often finds himself getting more touches on the ball, which results in marginally more shot attempts. In Doncic's absence, however, he seems more tentative, which is only exacerbated by the fact that he isn't utilized as much.

Kessler's impact as a lob threat was not as prominent against the Pistons on Sunday. While this could be attributed to Reaves' decision-making ability as a point guard, one could argue that getting the big man more involved offensively could help L.A. expand its scoring options.

A surprising development over the past few games has been Jarred Vanderbilt's increased involvement and playing time in the rotation. Before the start of the season, Vanderbilt was perceived as a clear trade option who may not have a place in the Lakers' current lineup. Now, however, he may have genuine upside.

As a scorer, Jarred Vanderbilt remains limited. With virtually no "bag" and a questionable shot from deep, he struggles to contribute consistently. However, it is becoming clear that his presence on the floor addresses the Lakers' need for rebounding.

On Sunday night, Vanderbilt was one of the most active players on the glass, securing 10 rebounds, two of which were offensive. Pairing this with his defensive effort and playmaking, and suddenly, the Lakers have another solid rotation piece.

At this juncture, Jarred Vanderbilt seems like a more impactful player than Sandro Mamukelashvili, who appears to be in a bit of a slump. Given Vanderbilt's defensive and rebounding upside, the Lakers could consider giving him more playing time depending on the matchups.

Aside from all the positive takeaways from Sunday night's game, the winning effort also highlighted an underlying issue with Quentin Grimes.

When the Purple and Gold signed him this summer, Quentin Grimes seemed like a promising addition with notable two-way upside. Having showcased his talents as a scorer during his stint with the Philadelphia 76ers, the fans had good reason to be excited. Unfortunately, it is safe to say that he hasn't lived up to expectations.

With Doncic sidelined, Grimes was promoted to the starting lineup with the intention of being a reliable second option next to Austin Reaves. While his early outburst of 10 points in the first quarter suggested that he was on track to fulfill this role, his offensive production came to a grinding halt by the second.

Before the game against Detroit, Grimes was averaging 7.6 points, 2.7 rebounds, and 2.7 assists per game while shooting 45.3% from the field and 21.4% shooting from deep. In many ways, this makes his 12-point outing look solid. Still, given his potential, along with his status as a key rotation piece, L.A. will need him to find his groove again.

#2791. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2789. Five-star Leipzig recover from Augsburg's fast start to extend perfect home record

Despite conceding goals in the first and last minutes of the game, RB Leipzig managed to maintain their perfect home record with a 5-2 win over FC Augsburg.

Augsburg scored the fastest goal in Bundesliga history during the Bavarian derby earlier this season, and they once again led inside the first minute against another Champions League club. #2786. Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch

Sixers-Cavs by the numbers: Pressure, threes and a 29-point rematch originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers and Cavaliers have already played one game that looked like two.

Cleveland led by 29 points during the Sixers' home opener on Oct. 22 and carried a 69-45 advantage into halftime. Philadelphia responded by winning the second half, 69-37, and completing a 114-106 comeback.

It was the Sixers' largest comeback since the NBA began maintaining detailed play-by-play data in 1997-98. Only nine other teams during that span had overcome a deficit of at least 29 points.

Fifteen days later, the teams will meet again Friday night at Rocket Arena. This time, the matchup counts toward the NBA Cup standings.

The 8-1 Sixers have won five consecutive games and opened group play with a 98-93 victory over the Knicks. The 4-4 Cavs have dropped two straight and will make their Cup debut.

Here are five numbers that help frame the rematch.

Philadelphia's comeback required more than a hot shooting stretch.

The Sixers committed 15 turnovers in the first half, allowing Cleveland to build a 22-6 advantage in points off turnovers. Their pressure then changed the game after halftime.

The Cavs committed 21 turnovers by the end of the night. What had been a 16-point Cleveland advantage in points off turnovers finished even at 22 apiece.

"They upped their pressure and physicality," Cavs head coach Kenny Atkinson said after the game. "It was pretty clear. We did not handle it well."

The Sixers scored 64 of their 69 second-half points through their starting lineup. Tyrese Maxey finished with 28 points, and Philadelphia turned a game that appeared finished into a franchise-record comeback.

The first half of the opening meeting now looks like an exception.

The Sixers have committed 12.9 turnovers per 100 possessions this season, the NBA's second-lowest rate. They are also third in turnover differential, averaging 3.4 fewer giveaways than their opponents.

That is notable for a team that entered the season intending to play faster. Philadelphia has pushed the ball without routinely surrendering possessions.

LeBron James' presence as the primary facilitator has helped. So has Maxey's willingness to move the ball when defenses trap him. Joel Embiid has averaged 5.2 assists, third among qualified centers, and the Sixers have increasingly trusted VJ Edgecombe to make decisions late in games.

Cleveland's defense has not forced many mistakes. The Cavs enter Friday near the bottom of the league in opponent turnover rate.

The first meeting swung when the Sixers stopped giving away possessions and forced Cleveland into mistakes of its own.

The Sixers have allowed 105.1 points per 100 possessions, the NBA's second-best defensive rating. Cleveland enters with the league's ninth-ranked offense.

The Cavs are the only top-10 offense the Sixers have faced through their first nine games.

Philadelphia has held opponents to a 50 percent effective field-goal percentage and 31.8 percent shooting from three-point range. Its defensive shot profile has been encouraging, too. Only 46 percent of opposing three-point attempts have been classified as wide open, the fifth-lowest share in the league, and opponents have taken just 22 percent of their threes from the corners.

There is reason for caution. Based on the quality of shots Philadelphia has allowed, only Washington's opponents have underperformed their expected effective field-goal percentage by a wider margin. The Sixers have defended well, but opponents are unlikely to remain this cold indefinitely.

Cleveland, with Donovan Mitchell, James Harden and Evan Mobley, can test whether those numbers hold against high-level individual shot creation.

The Cavs have attempted exactly 42 three-pointers per game and made 37.2 percent of them.

Cleveland went 7 for 29 from beyond the arc in Philadelphia. Mitchell, Harden and De'Andre Hunter combined to shoot 3 for 17. The Cavs scored 60 points in the paint and still reached 106, but their 24.1 percent performance from three prevented them from stopping the Sixers' comeback.

Friday's game could look considerably different if Cleveland generates its normal volume.

The Sixers have controlled the three-point line well so far, but Mitchell is coming off a 27-point performance against Oklahoma City and scored 32 in the first meeting with Philadelphia. Harden added 16 points that night, although his six turnovers became costly as the Sixers increased their pressure.

Philadelphia has been outrebounded by 5.1 per game. The Cavs rank sixth with 47.5 rebounds per contest.

The Sixers have already shown they can survive a major deficit on the glass. They beat Milwaukee by 11 despite getting outrebounded by 22. That is not a formula they will want to test regularly against Cleveland's size.

Mobley had 18 points and eight rebounds in the first meeting. Jarrett Allen added 14 points and seven boards, helping Cleveland win the rebounding battle, 46-44.

Whether Allen plays Friday remains uncertain.

Cleveland listed Allen as questionable because of a right knee injury after he left Wednesday's loss to Oklahoma City in the first quarter. Allen collided knee-to-knee with Thunder forward Chet Holmgren and did not return.

The Cavs also listed Peyton Watson as questionable with right hamstring soreness and Tyrese Proctor as questionable with an ankle injury. Proctor left Wednesday's game during the fourth quarter. Jaylon Tyson is out with a right ankle sprain.

Allen's availability could significantly alter the matchup on the glass. If he sits, Thomas Bryant would likely handle more of the center minutes behind Mobley. If he plays, Cleveland will have the same starting frontcourt that outrebounded the Sixers during the first meeting.

Atkinson has emphasized the possession battle during Cleveland's two-game slide.

"You've got to get more shots than the other team," he said this week. "Rebounding is a big part of it."

That also carries added importance in the NBA Cup, where point differential is the second tiebreaker after head-to-head results.

Philadelphia enters Friday at 1-0 with a plus-five differential in East Group B. Miami is also 1-0 at plus-20, while Cleveland is playing its first group game.

The Sixers already know they can erase 29 points against the Cavs. A cleaner start would be far more useful than proving it twice.

#2784. DeMarvion Overshown had a bad moment and didn’t even play

Culture was a buzzword around Brian Schottenheimer. Over the summer and through training camp, many people noted the culture that the head coach was building for and through the Dallas Cowboys.

That, obviously, has felt a bit hollow as losses have piled up this season, but on Sunday, during a win, it was on full display in the worst possible way.

In case you have no idea what is happening here, allow me to explain:

The Cowboys were holding on to a 20-17 lead against Indianapolis

It looked like the Colts were about to score

Quinnen Williams forced a fumble from Jonathan Taylor of all people

Shemar James recovered it and took it to the Dallas 30-yard line

The video in question is the Cowboys defense celebrating. Why would they not? They just took the football away at a critical moment. Their hard work was worthy of jubilation.

But someone else enters the frame. You can see someone, as the official described to be in sweats while assessing the penalty, run into the celebration. That was DeMarvion Overshown and as he was not a part of the team for the game (he was inactive) he was assessed with an unsportsmanlike conduct penalty.

This was the case because the celebration was not happening on the sideline where Cowboys players and staffers happened to be. You can see in the video that the action is quite literally in the middle of the field. Somehow, some way, Overshown ran out that far into the field and backed the team up 15 yards as a result.

Quite simply, this cannot happen. Overshown has been around for a while and is regarded to be among the team's leaders. His inability to stay healthy is an issue, but he has always been seen as someone who at the very least is great from a leadership standpoint. This certainly was not that.

Consider that the Cowboys did wind up getting those 15 yards back right away, but they punted on the ensuing possession and the Colts went on to tie the game up before eventually taking the lead that Dallas took back once and for all. But maybe the lack of penalty yards could have helped Dallas put the game away sooner.

The point isn't to live in hypotheticals. The point is that Overshown directly impacted his team's ability to win and he did so while not even a part of the game.

Brian Schottenheimer has to take care of that kind of thing.

#2783. Patrice Bergeron Shares Emotional Message to Former Bruins Teammates

BOSTON, MASSACHUSETTS - APRIL 26: Patrice Bergeron #37 of the Boston Bruins looks on against the Florida Panthers during the first period in Game Five of the First Round of the 2023 Stanley Cup Playoffs at TD Garden on April 26, 2023 in Boston, Massachusetts. (Photo by Maddie Meyer/Getty Images) Getty

As part of the Hockey Hall of Fame's Class of 2026, longtime Boston Bruins forward Patrice Bergeron was among the players selected to be forever enshrined in hockey immortality in Toronto.

One of the greatest players in Bruins history, Bergeron was also known for his humble nature despite his elite defensive skills and status as one of the NHL's greatest two-way forwards. He won numerous individual accolades throughout his career and helped the Bruins win the Stanley Cup in 2011, while also making two additional Stanley Cup Final appearances in 2013 and 2019.

During the 2026 Induction weekend in Toronto, Bergeron interviewed with NHL Insider James Duthie to talk about his career, and he was sure to give credit to the organization and his former teammates for helping him along in his Hall of Fame journey.

GettyBOSTON, MASSACHUSETTS – APRIL 26: Patrice Bergeron #37 of the Boston Bruins looks on against the Florida Panthers during the first period in Game Five of the First Round of the 2023 Stanley Cup Playoffs at TD Garden on April 26, 2023 in Boston, Massachusetts. (Photo by Maddie Meyer/Getty Images)

While interviewing with Duthie, Bergeron was sure to credit his family, friends, and former teammates for helping to make his Hall of Fame induction possible.

"It means a lot, but it also means a lot to share it with family and friends," Bergeron said. "But of course, my teammates and the organization. The Bruins have been so loyal to me in my career, and you can never get this accomplishment alone. To me, I guess it's a reflection of everyone that has helped me a long the way and sacrificed so much for me to be in this position. I'm very humbled and grateful, and obviously, a huge honor."

When asked if he always possessed natural leadership traits, Bergeron said that he felt it's part of his makeup, but also credited the numerous Bruins legends that were routinely around the organization, along with a few of his former on-ice teammates in Zdeno Chara and Mark Recchi.

"I think it's a little bit of both. You want to be yourself, and you want to speak from the heart and lead by example," he said. "I guess that was always in me I guess in a way, but also, you learn from a lot of guys. I was lucky to play in an organization with so much history and so many great leaders before us. Ray Bourque, John Bucyk, Cam Neely, and all these guys that were around when I was there. Obviously, to learn from, but then guys I played with – Zdeno Chara, I was 21 years old when he came in.

Obviously, his discipline and his work ethic, everything I was able to learn from and helped me a lot. Mark Recchi helped me find my voice around 2010 when he showed up with us…he helped me tremendously to take the next step in my leadership role."

During his career, Bergeron won the Selke Trophy as the NHL's best defensive forward six times, which he said came from his desire to do the right things to win hockey games rather than pad his stats.

"I thought the priority for me was to win hockey games, and I felt the best way and best chances for us to win was to do these types of things and worry about details," Bergeron said. "Ultimately, that's what led to results."

This article was originally published on HEAVY

#2777. Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls

Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Still no losing streaks to speak of for the 2026-27 Sixers.

The Sixers earned a 127-121 win Sunday over the Bulls to wrap up a 2-1 road trip. They moved to 9-2 overall and Chicago fell to 1-8.

Every Sixers starter scored in double figures.

Three reached the 20-point mark in Jaylen Brown (29 points), Tyrese Maxey (27 points) and LeBron James (22). Joel Embiid had 18 points and 10 rebounds.

Josh Giddey posted 19 points, 15 assists and seven rebounds for the Bulls.

The Sixers were without Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Bulls' absences included Norman Powell (right shoulder inflammation/soreness) and Zach Collins (respiratory illness).

Next on the Sixers' schedule is a home game Tuesday night vs. the Celtics. Here are observations on their win over Chicago:

Following his 0-for-9 night Friday in the Sixers' loss to the Cavaliers, Embiid did not snap right back into form.

He was well short on his first shot attempt, a wide-open three-pointer. Embiid then failed to score in the post over Jalen Smith.

Embiid got a field goal to go at the 6:42 mark of the first quarter when he tipped in his own miss. However, Smith canned a three on the ensuing possession to give Chicago a 23-16 lead.

James committed two early turnovers, although he did sink a corner three-pointer during a fast-paced first quarter. VJ Edgecombe added two long-range jumpers in the first.

Entering Sunday, the Sixers' minus-9.6 net rating in the first quarter ranked 27th in the NBA. They managed to hold a one-point lead in Chicago after the first.

The Sixers' offensive rebounding was their greatest strength in the first quarter. They poured in 17 second-chance points. Embiid had four offensive boards in the opening frame.

Jabari Walker assumed backup center duties for the second straight game without Bona. He picked up where Embiid left off on the glass, grabbing seven rebounds (three offensive) in a great first stint.

Though he's undersized in the height department for an NBA center at 6-foot-7, Walker has plenty of rebounding tools.

He's listed at a strong 237 pounds, doesn't mind contact whatsoever, hunts the ball, and has a knack for winning contested boards. Walker showed all of that Sunday. Very hard to overstate how well he rebounded.

The Sixers' first-shot offense also had many bright moments in the second quarter.

James got rolling on his way to a 16-point first half, Embiid made a couple of mid-range jumpers, and Maxey hit two free throws put the Sixers up 60-50 late in the second period.

Chicago evened the game at 68-all early in the third quarter on a Matas Buzelis three, but the Bulls couldn't sustain that run.

The Bulls' defense continued to look like one of the worst in the NBA and the Sixers' well-balanced offense kept the scoreboard moving. Brown nailed a turnaround jumper to give his team a double-digit lead. Walker knocked down a top-of-the-key triple to extend the Sixers' advantage to 12 points.

The Sixers had two apparent injury scares in the second half. Brown limped to the sideline with 6:55 to go in the third quarter, but he stayed in the game. Edgecombe headed to the locker room after subbing out at the 9:08 mark of the fourth quarter. He returned to the Sixers' bench and ultimately returned for a final stint.

On the floor, the Sixers couldn't ease to victory. Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson trimmed Chicago's deficit to 116-113 by draining a three. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse called timeout.

The Sixers were efficient enough in the half court down the stretch.

Embiid made a put-back hoop and a pair of free throws. Maxey drilled a side-step three-ball after a wraparound feed from James. The Sixers converted their clutch free throws and secured a winning road trip.

#2776. Clippers beat Pelicans to end 7-game losing streak

INGLEWOOD, Calif. (AP) — Darius Garland had 24 points and 10 assists, Brook Lopez added 20 points and the Los Angeles Clippers ended a seven-game losing streak with a 139-123 victory over the New Orleans Pelicans on Sunday night.

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

INDIANAPOLIS – The Indianapolis Colts fought their way back from down 10 in the second half and took control late in the fourth quarter, but the thrill of stealing a comeback win was short-lived as Dak Prescott led the Dallas Cowboys on a game-winning touchdown drive inside the final minute to escape with a 27-23 win.

Aubrey broke the ice with a 45-yard field goal on Dallas' opening drive, but finished 2-for-3 on Sunday. Shrader connected on field goals from 47, 59 and 50-yards out, but his go-ahead field goal to give the Colts a late lead would not be sustained.

Colts edge rusher Laiatu Latu raced around the edge of left tackle Tyler Guyton, got a firm grasp of Prescott twice from behind, and knocked the ball loose for a strip-sack. Rookie edge rusher Caden Curry recovered the fumble and returned it inside the red zone to give Indianapolis an early takeaway. Curry, a Greenwood, Ind. native, had a breakout performance and later secured his first sack in his first NFL start.

The Colts leaned on their ground game as rookie running back Seth McGowan finished the six-play drive with a 2-yard touchdown to take an early 7-3 lead. McGowan provided a spark for the rushing attack, finishing with 45 rushing yards on just six carries, less than half of the carries of Indy's workhorse.

Dallas answered with a methodical, 18-play scoring drive. Toward the end of the first quarter, an unnecessary roughness penalty from rookie linebacker C.J. Allen gifted Dallas a first down. Faced with fourth-and-2 from inside the 10, Prescott laid a perfect pass on a corner near the back pylon to tight end Jake Ferguson, who rose above the defender to haul in a touchdown and put the Cowboys back ahead 10-7.

Indianapolis struggled to generate any offense during the first half as quarterback Daniel Jones was held to just 50 passing yards, while Dallas' pass rush found a way to wreak havoc in the pocket. Cowboys rookie safety Caleb Downs blitzed untouched for a blindside sack to force another punt and had a breakout performance in his first matchup against his older brother, Josh.

Four consecutive possessions for Indy in the second quarter resulted in a punt, controlling just six minutes on the game clock for a total of 40 net yards. The Cowboys closed the first half with a pair of deep shots to receiver CeeDee Lamb, with the latter striking for a 44-yard touchdown to increase the lead to 17-7. Prescott recognized the Cover 2 zone, found Lamb with his hand raised and fired a missile to beat the defender for the explosive score. Lamb was virtually unstoppable, hauling in 10 catches for 195 receiving yards and a touchdown.

Jones' stats were inflated late in the third after he hit receiver Ashton Dulin on a screen, then the speedster broke through the initial tackle before racing 55 yards into the end zone. The most explosive offensive play for the Colts brought the score to within 20-17 and injected life into a restless crowd that had watched its offense struggle through nearly three quarters.

Perhaps the most momentum-shifting play happened early in the fourth. Faced with 3rd-and-12, Prescott stayed in the pocket and took a big hit, but found a wide open Lamb diving to secure the catch inside of Colts territory. The All-Pro receiver was never touched or down by contact, so he got up and tried to race for the end zone, but lost the football as the turf knocked the ball out of his grasp, allowing Cam Bynum to recover and seize the momentum. Lamb was initially ruled down by contact, but Colts head coach Shane Steichen challenged the play to reverse the ruling.

The Colts chance to capitalize unraveled after a 40-yard defensive pass interference penalty against Bland moved Indianapolis into scoring position. Running back Jonathan Taylor was stuffed behind the line of scrimmage by defensive tackle Quinnen Williams and coughed up the football, wasting another scoring opportunity as linebacker Shemar James recovered the lost fumble.

Midway through the fourth, McGowan broke free on a left zone run for a 36-yard gain to breach Cowboys territory. Shrader delivered on a 59-yard field goal to level the score at 20, then later added a 50-yarder to take a late 23-20 lead with just 1:37 left to play.

On the very first play of Dallas' final true drive, Prescott launched another missile downfield and receiver Ryan Flournoy hauled in the 46-yard catch over cornerback Sauce Gardner to instantly set the Cowboys inside the red zone. After consecutive Colts penalties set Dallas up at the 1-yard-line, running back Javante Williams plunged into the end zone from 1-yard out to score the go-ahead touchdown with just 1:08 remaining.

Despite Dallas' offense piling up 508 total yards, Indy still had a chance to get one final stop with the lead. Instead, the Colts allowed the Cowboys to reclaim the lead in less than 30 seconds. The Colts final drive lasted just three plays, featuring intentional grounding and another incompletion before Jones' lofted an awful pass over the middle of the field, which Caleb Downs picked off to terminate the last chance to steal a victory.

Taylor was held to just 46 yards on 13 carries. Jones finished 20-of-34 for 180 passing yards, with one touchdown and one interception. For Indianapolis, the comeback demonstrated the ability to fight back when the game appeared out of reach, but multiple defensive lapses, along with a pair of costly turnovers ultimately overshadowed the effort. The stampede once again left the stadium to boo's, listening to "Here we go" cadences, and "How 'bout them Cowboys" chants.

The Colts host the 1-8 Miami Dolphins next Sunday inside Lucas Oil Stadium, with kickoff scheduled for 1 p.m.

#2770. From Madrid to Mainz: Lennart Karl strikes again as Bayern extend unbeaten run

Bayern Munich maintained their unbeaten start to the Bundesliga season with a 2-1 victory away to Mainz 05. Goals from Joshua Kimmich and Lennart Karl put the visitors in control, although a late strike from substitute Philipp Tietz ensured a nervy finish.

The Bavarians ultimately secured all three points to head into the international break on a positive note.

Here is our official match report for the game:

Mainz had a little extra motivation heading into Saturday's clash. Urs Fischer, who made his debut as Mainz coach in last season's 2-2 draw against Bayern, was hoping to cause another upset. After all, the hosts had won four of their previous six home meetings with the record champions - a remarkable record against the Bundesliga giants.

Fischer also had a special message for Nadiem Amiri. Left out of Germany coach Jürgen Klopp's squad for the upcoming Nations League fixtures, the Mainz boss encouraged his star midfielder to use the disappointment as motivation and "show everyone what he's capable of".

Unfortunately for the hosts, Bayern had other plans.

The visitors dominated possession and looked the more dangerous side from the opening minutes. Mainz tried to disrupt their rhythm and create opportunities through set pieces, but Bayern eventually found a way through in the 14th minute.

A misplaced pass in Mainz's build-up handed Harry Kane the opportunity to launch a counterattack. As Sano stepped out to challenge the Englishman, Joshua Kimmich was left completely unmarked in the centre. Ballon d'Or winner Kane picked him out perfectly and the Bayern star made no mistake, smashing the ball underneath the crossbar to put the visitors 1-0 ahead.

Mainz thought they had found their equaliser shortly before the break. Amiri delivered a dangerous free-kick into the box, Jonathan Tah got the slightest touch, and Manuel Neuer missed his attempt to reach the ball. Lee was waiting at the back post and headed it over the line.

However, the celebrations were short-lived. An offside position in the build-up ruled out the goal.

Neuer appeared to pick up an injury during the set-piece incident and was unable to continue. Jonas Urbig replaced the veteran goalkeeper before half-time.

The second half initially offered little in terms of excitement, but Bayern remained firmly in control.

Then came the 57th minute. Konrad Laimer spotted Harry Kane making a run in behind and delivered a superb long ball towards the striker. Kane got his shot away, but after the rebound fell back into his path, he cleverly squared it for Lennart Karl.

The teenager made no mistake, firing home to double Bayern's advantage.

After finding the net against Atlético Madrid in the Champions League, Karl was at it again – this time in Mainz. Not a bad week for the young Bayern sensation!

The goal put Bayern in a comfortable position for the remainder of the match. The visitors appeared to tire somewhat, particularly given their demanding schedule and recent injury concerns.

Bayern appeared to have the game under control in the closing stages, but Mainz made a late attempt to turn things around. Substitute Phillip Tietz pulled one back for the hosts in the 92nd minute, setting up a tense finish. However, the late goal proved insufficient to prevent Bayern from taking all three points.

The victory provides a welcome boost for the visitors ahead of the international break while extending their unbeaten run in the Bundesliga.

Mainz, meanwhile, will have two weeks to regroup and address their recent struggles as they look to get their league campaign back on track.

#2769. Kirk Cousins becomes an option for a contender, like the Vikings

With a switch from veteran quarterback Kirk Cousins to rookie Fernando Mendoza seemingly inevitable, the question becomes whether the Raiders will explore trading Cousins to a quarterback-needy contender.

At the top of the list, obviously, would be the Vikings, where Cousins played from 2018 through 2023.

The Raiders have pushed the implausible idea that they “had not received any trade inquiries” regarding Cousins entering this weekend. This time of year, the inquiries are rampant. Everyone is poking and prodding in an effort to see who’s available and what it would take to get them.

It would be malpractice not to make the call. For all anyone knows, the Raiders wouldn’t want much for him. There’s only one way to find out.

The Raiders also are putting out the word that they aren’t interested in trading him. Beyond the fact that there’s no reason to have interest if no one has called, it feels like an effort by the Raiders to operate from a position of strength.

Surely, there’s an offer for Cousins that they wouldn’t reject. By acting coy about possibly trading Cousins, the Raiders’ obvious goal is to make it known that it won’t be cheap.

If they’ll be pivoting to Mendoza in any event, why not trade Cousins? They have Aidan O’Connell, who has 17 career starts, to serve as the understudy to Mendoza.

That doesn’t mean anyone will make the Raiders an offer they’d accept. But it would be lunacy for a team like the Vikings, who have a Super Bowl-ready defense, to not at least inquire.

Chances are they already have. And, if they haven’t, chances are they will before Tuesday at 4:00 p.m. ET. Especially after getting thumped on Sunday in their return to the Bay Area.

#2767. Fantasy Football: Backup QBs get their chance, while Jordan Love shows how it's done

For the last week, I've been playing armchair GM. I drafted fake NFL trades. I mapped out which backups would inherit a role once their teammate got shipped out. I had a few stashes picked out for when the dominoes started falling on Tuesday.

The trade deadline is still looming in the background. Some of those moves will happen, and we'll sort through the fallout next week. But the games had other plans. Injuries reshuffled depth charts faster than any front office could. And a few players reminded us that while we're busy speculating about who's going where, the guys on the field still decide what happens.

That's the funny thing about fantasy. We can spend all week preparing for one storyline, and the biggest news comes from somewhere else entirely. So, let's set the rumors aside for a moment and dig into what Sunday actually told us.

Injuries and some bad QB play brought four new signal-callers into our lives in Week 9. Well, at least new to the 2026 season, because we've seen or have narratives on each of these guys from years past. But in a couple of cases, holding onto those priors may have been poor process. Take Shedeur Sanders' day for instance.

I feel like I got the best of both worlds here. My Bengals got a win while Cleveland lost, and Sanders kept the Browns' passing game alive. And credit to the sophomore passer. While mostly completing passes in the short area of the field, he was slinging the ball in every direction and distance. His final two drives were everything you'd want out of a backup.

However, before folks start whispering "QB controversy," I'd have them look under the hood regarding Sanders' output.

EPA per Dropback: -0.21 (2025 Sanders), -0.05 (Week 9 Sanders), -0.03 (Watson)

Passing Success Rate: 34.6%, 33.3%, 44.0%

Pressure-to-Sack Rate: 17.5%, 31.3%, 25.4%

So, two things can be true at once. In the right matchup, Cleveland's pass-catching nucleus of Denzel Boston (6-98-0), KC Concepcion (4-85-1) and Harold Fannin Jr. (2-42-1) can make a QB look great in the box score. But that doesn't mean that they were. Sanders had a -6.7% CPOE on the day. And before you blame the Browns' offensive line, Sanders' three-second average time to throw is the third longest of either Cleveland QB through Week 9. Two of his five sacks came on third down, ending drives. And seeing Sanders sitting at 9.3 YAC per completion (ninth-most of any starting QB this season) lends credence to the idea that the Browns' receivers did the heavy lifting.

Even if Sanders keeps the job, unless he faces the Saints every week, we shouldn't expect anything different out of this offense than if Watson were under center.

I could say the same about the Eagles switching up their QB1.

A positive from Joe Milton's first start was that we saw flashes of the old Eagles offense (complimentary). Milton's nine touches and 64 yards on the ground were the most of any Philly QB all season. But past that, we saw more of the same offense we've seen throughout the entire season (derogatory).

Of course, operating behind a reconstructed offensive line without your WR1 would put any QB in a bad spot. Five of Milton's seven sacks came on pressures under 2.5 seconds. But he threw short of the sticks on a third of his third-down attempts. Just six of his throws of double-digit air yards turned into completions. Milton did get to show off his cannon of an arm on a few throws. But without much talent around him, opposing GMs should be giving the Eagles a call before Tuesday afternoon.

Coincidentally, one of the last games of the afternoon featured the remaining two backup-turned-starter QBs.

Well, there you have it. Drew Lock's TD pass to AJ Barner was the only passing score in the game. Between Lock and Gardner Minshew, the two journeyman QBs totaled 341 yards. Hopefully, you weren't stuck with either as a streaming option. But most were looking to their skill players for production. And at least with Seattle, they kept to the status quo.

Target Share (Smith-Njigba): 36.4%, 35.7%

Sure, we'd all be feeling better if Jaxon Smith-Njigba came down in the end zone with the ball instead of Barner. But Lock was, well, locked in on No. 11. His first five passes went toward his WR1. The only real change was a shift in the backfield rotation.

Charbonnet: 35.3% (Week 8 Rushing Share), 64.0% (Week 9 Rushing Share)

Jadarian Price was able to partially salvage his day with a 4-54-0 stat line as a receiver. But Zach Charbonnet was on the field for 42.9% of Seattle's red-zone snaps while Price was down at 14.3%. Charbonnet was also the preferred short-yardage back, converting two of three attempts. Luckily, the Seahawks face the Raiders next week, who are allowing a league-high 132.1 YPG to opposing RBs. It sets up for both RBs to succeed, but without a defined split, we'll need at least another week to figure out who's the best start in Seattle's backfield.

On the flip side of that same game, the Cardinals took the progressive approach to play-calling.

Arizona was at a +5.0% PROE against a coverage unit allowing the second-fewest passing YPG. Garnder Minshew attempted two more passes against Seattle than Jacoby Brissett back in Week 2. Surprisingly, Minshew notched the fifth-best passing success rate against Seattle through nine games. More importantly, he kept his eyes on the only two pass-catchers we care about.

Trey McBride: 27.6% (Target Rate), 2.0 (Yards per Route Run)

The only concern is the Cardinals' play volume. After averaging 69.6 plays per game, Arizona's time between plays was up, and its total plays were down to 51. At least with Brissett, the increased attempts would allow either McBride or Wilson to rack up yards. In this version, we're asking for high-end efficiency from Minshew. And against the Rams and Chiefs over the next two weeks, that might be too tall of a request.

Before I get into the Broncos, I wanted to give a quick salute to Bryce Young and the Panthers. After two weeks with multiple sacks and at least one turnover, Carolina found its rhythm on offense again. Young was decisive with the ball (2.47 time to throw), and his completion percentage was back up over 70% for the first time since Week 4. Accordingly, shifting our focus to Denver, the Panthers' resurgence forced Denver to keep their foot on the gas.

Bo Nix's passing aDOT was over five air yards for just the fourth time this season. And that's after losing Jaylen Waddle, who was playing well, in the first quarter. With the Broncos trailing after the third quarter, Sunday was the third instance where Nix had double-digit attempts to close a game. Pat Bryant (14.7% target rate) and Troy Franklin (11.8%) fell into their old roles as intermediate options. However, the only player who should be on our fantasy radar from Denver was in the backfield.

So we're two weeks into Jonah Coleman's return to the lineup. Along with RJ Harvey being on IR, the Broncos' backfield has condensed to two RBs. But looking at Coleman's usage rates across both games, Denver's really down to just one rusher.

(Coleman) Rushing Share: 61.1% (Week 8), 81.0% (Week 9)

Over the last two weeks, Coleman has been the guy in every situation that's correlated to fantasy scoring. J.K. Dobbins hasn't taken a snap on third or fourth downs. When Nix has targeted his RBs, the rookie has secured all but two attempts. And when Denver gets into scoring position, Coleman has out-touched the veteran almost 3:1. The Broncos are heading into the bye week, but they face the Raiders (who just gave up 157 all-purpose yards to the 49ers' RBs) twice over their next five games.

You'll have to overpay to secure him, but this week will be your last chance to get Coleman on your squad.

The story from Sunday was Patrick Mahomes' elbow injury. In the moment, the comparisons to Brock Purdy's UCL tear from '22 were at the forefront of everybody's minds. But Mahomes not only came back to the game, but also spoke to the media about his situation after the game.

None of this sounds great. But after seeing images of Mahomes cradling his arm, we'll take what we can get. Besides, the play-calling and pass-game execution already favored a QB with an injured throwing arm.

Passing aDOT: 3.1 (pre-injury), 2.8 (post-injury)

Mahomes' passing chart against the Jets looks like a normal image from earlier in his career, just compressed. He threw two passes that traveled 10 or more air yards in the second half. Mahomes is now on a two-game streak with zero completions on intermediate or deep throws. On the one hand, having one of the greatest passers reduced to a dink-and-dunk QB would be great for players like Rashee Rice (24.1% target share) or Travis Kelce (20.7%). But they're not the only ones benefiting from Mahomes keeping it short.

Kenneth Walker III watched Emmett Johnson create explosives as a receiver in Week 8 and decided to get back into the action against the Jets.

Walker had 41 receiving yards. Forty-four of them came after the catch. Re-read those last two sentences. No, that's not a typo.

Because half of his targets came behind the LoS, Walker had more YAC than total yards through the air. In either case, because Walker re-established his efficiency as a pass-catcher while maintaining his dominance over the backfield, we should expect to see him in the RB1 conversation for the rest of the season.

Not to say I know a ton about QB mechanics, but you pick stuff up over the years. Like how to set your feet, generate torque through your hips and drive the football. Essentially, there's a theoretical way to do it. Jordan Love has his own approach.

Drifting back in the pocket. Throwing off his back foot. Releasing a pass that looks like a meatball sub for the defense … until the camera pans far enough to see a Packer receiver in perfect position to come down with the ball. Green Bay's highlights had it all. Love and HC Matt LaFleur watched Cincinnati's offense turn its pass-game triumvirate into gods in the morning on Week 9 and, not to be outdone, decided they'd put on a similar show in the afternoon.

I reference passing success rate a lot, but it's a gauge of how often a QB is keeping their offense on schedule in terms of yardage gained or EPA generated on a specific down. Love had 18 of those throws against the Patriots; 15 of them went to Matthew Golden and Christian Watson. Love completed 13 passes that turned into first downs or TDs. Nos. 9 and 0 accounted for 12. Unless you rostered and started Josh Jacobs, you didn't notice he left early with a fractured finger, and the Packers' running game was back to where it was before he rejoined the team.

Love's toughest test will be Green Bay's divisional rematch against the Vikings in Week 10. After its bye, three of its next five opponents are in the top 10 in fantasy PPG allowed to QBs. If you're looking to make a push into the playoffs, swinging for one of the main pieces of the Packers' passing game will help secure you a spot.

#2764. The Lakers are apparently a good third quarter team now

For much of the LeBron James era, one of the defining characteristics of the team, more often than not, was how much they struggled in third quarters.

For some reason, the halftime adjustments discussed in the locker room needed some sort of nesting period before taking effect. More often than not, that meant the Lakers were playing from behind in the second half.

This season, though, the script has flipped and the Lakers are sailing unchartered waters. Albeit with a small sample size, not only are the Lakers no longer a bad third quarter team, they're best team in the league out of the locker room.

That was evidenced to some degree in the team's recent win over the Blazers. After spending much of the first half missing seemingly every open shot possible, the Lakers came out of the locker room with a big run in the third period to take control of the game.

"I wouldn't call it adjustments," Redick said of his halftime talk on Friday. "We cleaned up what we talked about. This is becoming a theme. It's just like, I think the guys are doing a great job of responding at halftime. We've had really good third quarters. I just, at some point, I hope we're better with our execution on the defensive end to start the game. But they always, so far this year, they've responded nearly every time at halftime to just cleaning things up."

In that game on Friday, the Lakers showed composure to keep plugging away despite what they were doing not producing results in the first half. Eventually, all those open shots they created started to fall and the tides turned, leading to a victory.

"We just stuck to the game plan," Austin Reaves said of the approach in the second half. "We talked about it in timeouts, halftime, for us to only be down seven with the lack of shots that were made, it was kind of impressive, honestly. To be in a game like that against a really good team, it was just stay the course. We're going to make shots and, obviously, Luka doing what he does willed us to that win."

Ideally, the Lakers wouldn't need to be a great third quarter team and can just be a consistent force throughout all four quarters. It's also not like the team is bad in the first half as they rank eighth in net rating in the first quarter and sixth in net rating in the second quarter.

It's just that their net rating in the third quarter of 29.7 is 12 points higher than any other team in the period. The fourth quarter is the only frame where they don't rank in the top ten, though it's a figure that could be influenced negatively by some pretty woeful late-game sequences against the Clippers and Raptors.

Through 10 games, everything can be chalked up to a small sample size, so it'll be interesting to see if this is a trend that will continue. But it certainly was startling, if also true, to see Redick call the Lakers a good third quarter team after Friday's win.

#2762. Should Giants trade for Jonathan Taylor? What's next for Kirk Cousins, Chiefs WRs? NFL trade deadline questions emerge from Sunday's games

The conversation began two minutes into the second half.

The New York Giants' running backs had carried the ball eight times for a net 8 yards at that point. They'd traversed negative 20 yards before contact, according to the broadcast's Next Gen Stats graphic.

So Fox color analyst Greg Olsen, who spent 14 years as an NFL tight end, knew the question fans would ask themselves: Should the Giants, leading a struggling NFC East, trade for a veteran running back?

The club had been linked to Indianapolis Colts running back Jonathan Taylor.

"I think they got to press pause here and take a clean look at it," Olsen said.

"If you get hit in the backfield," play-by-play announcer Joe Davis agreed, "it doesn't matter who you are."

Olsen contextualized the Giants' running back interest against the Eagles signing Saquon Barkley in 2024.

"I like to say: Good backs don't make bad run games good," Olsen said. "Good backs — in Saquon's case, great backs — make good run games great. So typically you want to have the offensive line, you want to have the run scheme, you want to have that element, you want to have that box checked.

"Then you add a Jonathan Taylor or a Kenneth Walker or a Saquon Barkley. The examples of going that order of operations are the ones that typically work the best."

Whoever cuts film for the Giants leadership might want to include that audio.

The Giants have struggled to develop consistency in their run game this season as injuries have limited running backs Cam Skattebo, Tyrone Tracy Jr. and Devin Singletary. Add a season-ending injury to mobile starting quarterback Jaxson Dart and a benching earlier this season to Skattebo, and the Giants have compiled an effective rushing attack every other week this season. Their offensive line ranks 18th in run-block win rate, per ESPN's advanced metric.

On Sunday, facing an injured and reeling Eagles team without Skattebo (hamstring), the Giants rushed for 80 total yards including a combined 33 from quarterbacks J.J. McCarthy and Jameis Winston.

Would Taylor help? And perhaps more importantly: Is he worth the Christian McCaffrey-like return the Colts are seeking, which equates to roughly a 2027 second- , third- and fourth-round pick as well as a 2028 fifth?

"I'd say we're open for any kind of situation, but I will say this, things do change," Harbaugh said. "Things change from day to day. So it's not like one day you may do something and the next day something happens and it changes your position. I'm not saying that report's not true. Those conversations are taking place, and if something happens for any player, I can't talk about any player because they're with other teams right now.

"We're not ruling anything out, but I don't see anything particularly imminent right now either. So we'll see what happens."

Running back Jonathan Taylor's status will dominate the run-up to the NFL trading deadline on Tuesday.

Complicating matters might be McCarthy's health. McCarthy left the game in the third quarter with a hip injury suffered on a hit from linebacker Zack Baun, the quarterback returning but then limping after a scramble. He finished the game. But if the Giants' run game requires quarterback mobility to jell, is McCarthy — their current mobile option, with Dart out for the season — durable enough?

And if that's in question, is a haul of future draft capital worth spending during the first season of the Harbaugh era?

At 5-2-1, the Giants hold a comfortable 65% chance of making the playoffs, per Next Gen Stats.

But their wins have all come against sub-.500 teams. Their schedule will thicken after this week's Thursday division game against the Washington Commanders. Five of their following six opponents are currently better than .500.

Taylor may help, albeit without seeming necessary, for the Giants to win the division. Will an offense pairing Taylor with McCarthy or Winston, pending health, help the Giants win a playoff game? And if not, are the Giants better off re-exploring the position this spring, at a lower cost?

"Somebody's got to come out that you really want to trade for that makes sense," Harbaugh said. "It's got to make sense short term and long term. We're not just going to go for a short-term fix at an exorbitant cost …

"Usually when you do that sort of thing, it doesn't work out for you."

What else from Sunday may impact this week's trade deadline? Here are two more stories to keep an eye on:

At this point, it seems more likely than not that Cousins will stay put in Vegas and the Raiders will spare rookie quarterback Fernando Mendoza the gauntlet of Seattle Seahawks, Denver Broncos and Cleveland Browns defenses next on the Raiders' schedule.

Even after posting his worst game of the season in a Sunday loss to the San Francisco 49ers, Cousins is still valuable to head coach Klint Kubiak and the franchise. His 2,340 passing yards rank fifth in the league and his 97.1 passer rating ranks 12th.

The Raiders will not simply give away Cousins.

But after Monday night, a team could be increasingly eager to test the price.

A Vikings team with the No. 1-ranked scoring defense and No. 27-ranked scoring offense hosts a red-hot Buffalo Bills team on Monday night. Minnesota will have a chance to see the areas of its team that are ready to compete deep into the playoffs and the areas that are not.

To this point, offense — and especially quarterback — have been glaring weaknesses. Kyler Murray has completed 64.9% of pass attempts for 1,022 yards, five touchdowns and three interceptions while Carson Wentz has completed 59% for 276 yards, three touchdowns and no picks.

The Raiders, with Cousins, have posted 331 yards per game to the Vikings' 279.4. And Cousins already knows head coach Kevin O'Connell's Vikings system intimately after playing in it in 2022 and 2023.

One league source thought a third-round pick would compel the Raiders to send Cousins to Minnesota and move on to Mendoza, recouping capital for a player who won't net a compensatory pick if he leaves in the spring due to his contract structure. Two other league sources put Cousins' value as ranging from a fifth to a fourth, possibly with a conditional clause in the trade.

"That makes sense for them," a high-ranking AFC executive said of the Vikings. "It'd be low start-up cost because he understands the offense already. They have enough around him that it wouldn't be like he's shouldering everything.

"It shouldn't cost more than a fourth or a fifth given his age [38] and you're probably renting him. That's probably a fourth."

While Sunday marked Cousins' first game this year with more interceptions (three) than touchdowns (one), two league sources said they did not expect that to influence Cousins' value at all given his large sample size of work, the Vikings' familiarity with Cousins and the 49ers' known defensive strengths.

The Raiders slipping from playoff contention — they have just a 14% chance of making the postseason, per Next Gen Stats — could increase their interest in moving Cousins for the right price. Monday night's game could sway the Vikings' interest.

Two weeks ago, the leading Chiefs receiver was Cyrus Allen with 18 receiving yards. Last week, the leader in the room was Rashee Rice with 53 yards. And this week, in a 35-12 win over the New York Jets, Rice's 49 led the room.

The Chiefs need help. Manufacturing a passing game with tight ends and running backs will limit their ceiling in a postseason run. Add a hamstring injury to Xavier Worthy on Sunday, and the offense's reliance on running back Kenneth Walker III makes it too vulnerable.

Patrick Mahomes' reliance on 37-year-old tight end Travis Kelce is not sustainable. And after an MRI confirmed Mahomes' elbow injury was a strain or sprain in severity, per NFL Network, the Chiefs can feel comfortable moving forward with a trade.

The Chiefs haven't hidden their interest in a receiver. Nine weeks into the season, the case for a trade is strong. They're 6-2 with a 92% chance to make the playoffs, per Next Gen Stats. Their run game is potent, their offense the second-most productive in the league. Only eight teams have allowed fewer yards on defense than the Chiefs' 307.9 per game, per TruMediaSports.

Who might elevate the pass-catching group?

Patrick Mahomes needs more than Travis Kelce in the Chiefs' passing game.

The Jacksonville Jaguars' Brian Thomas Jr. likely offers the highest ceiling if Jacksonville is willing to trade him. That reality is unclear: While Thomas is averaging just 2.75 targets a game and playing just 43% of offensive snaps, the Jaguars might value him as a field-stretcher for now and insurance if their corps sustains an injury. League sources expect Thomas to cost roughly a third-round pick to compel the Jaguars to trade him, with that value ranging from a fourth to a second depending on how competitive the market is and whether the Jaguars could recoup something else they need (hello, defensive tackle) in return.

"If I'm them, I'm not giving away any talent, no matter how useful they are, as long as they're not a detriment from a behavior or execution standpoint," the high-ranking AFC executive said. "OK, he's underperforming being a first-round pick, fine. But you're an injury away from, OK, if he's not there, who's playing receiver?

"Your goals are much different than everybody else's. So I would only move that dude before the season is over … if the price is just too good."

Indianapolis Colts receiver Josh Downs and Tennessee Titans receiver Calvin Ridley appear to be lower-ceiling, available options. Cowboys receiver George Pickens should be worth calling on, though team owner Jerry Jones seems unlikely to budge. And then there's San Francisco 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk as well as former Chiefs and Dolphins receiver Tyreek Hill, who each have the potential to boom or bust with questions about their health and readiness to positively impact a team. Historically, the Chiefs have not been deterred by risk.
